also holds here: released Qwen3-4B degrades without collapse, whereas
the SFT-then-GRPO checkpoint collapses after OPSD. Their logs separate
early: entropy and gradient norm rise for the SFT-then-GRPO
initialization but remain bounded or decrease for released Qwen3-4B
([subsection 6.4](#S6.SS4 "6.4 OPSD Training Dynamics ‣ 6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

### C.7 OPSD versus GRPO on Released Qwen3 Thinking Models

For the OPSD–GRPO comparison in
[Table 6](#S5.T6 "Table 6 ‣ 5.7 Training-Compute Trade-off: OPSD versus GRPO ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
we train both methods from the released Qwen3-1.7B and Qwen3-4B models
in their native Th mode and evaluate after 50 updates. Both methods
consume exactly the same 3,200 examples from the OpenMathReasoning
integer-answer subset in the same order: we materialize the first 3,200
rows produced by the GRPO random sampler (seed 1) as the sequential OPSD
dataset. GRPO uses the final answer only for reward verification,
whereas OPSD supplies the corresponding post-thinking solution as
privileged teacher context.

The matched OPSD runs use a frozen same-model teacher, full-parameter
updates, a 1,024-token rollout cap, global batch size 64, learning rate
$`1\times 10^{-6}`$, forward KL, token-contribution clipping at 0.05,
and seed 42. Both model sizes train on 2 A800 GPUs; Qwen3-1.7B uses
per-device batch size 8 with gradient accumulation 4, and Qwen3-4B uses
per-device batch size 4 with gradient accumulation 8. The longest
rendered prompt in the materialized dataset is 2,584 tokens.
[subsection B.4](#A2.SS4 "B.4 GRPO Control Configuration ‣ Appendix B Experimental Setup ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
gives the GRPO configuration, and
[Table 63](#A7.T63 "Table 63 ‣ G.1.9 SFT Initialization Sweep ‣ G.1 Main-Text Experiment Breakdowns ‣ Appendix G Per-Dataset Evaluation Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
and
[Table 62](#A7.T62 "Table 62 ‣ G.1.9 SFT Initialization Sweep ‣ G.1 Main-Text Experiment Breakdowns ‣ Appendix G Per-Dataset Evaluation Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
report the benchmark-level results.

The cost column in
[Table 6](#S5.T6 "Table 6 ‣ 5.7 Training-Compute Trade-off: OPSD versus GRPO ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
uses training-loop time through the evaluated checkpoint and excludes
evaluation and data preprocessing. The results support the trade-off in
[subsection 5.7](#S5.SS7 "5.7 Training-Compute Trade-off: OPSD versus GRPO ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
neither method dominates accuracy, while OPSD uses substantially fewer
GPU-hours under these recipes. At 1.7B, OPSD has higher Avg@8 and equal
Pass@8; at 4B, GRPO leads Avg@8 and OPSD leads Pass@8.

## Appendix D Supplementary Analysis Results

### D.1 Token-Level Analysis Details

This subsection reports the protocol and per-model results underlying
[section 6](#S6 "6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models").
Unless noted otherwise, the analysis uses 2,048 prompts with two
on-policy rollouts each; the position-window study uses a 6,144-token
rollout cap.

#### D.1.1 Token-Level Analysis Protocol

We compute the token-level statistics in
[section 6](#S6 "6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
offline from the same student and teacher distributions used in
training. For each initial checkpoint, we sample 2,048 training prompts
with two on-policy rollouts each ($`n=4{,}096`$) at temperature $`1.1`$
and a 1,024-token rollout cap. The frozen self-teacher scores the
visited response tokens under its privileged context. Reported forward
KL is the vocabulary-summed, unclipped $`\beta=0`$ divergence. Unless
explicitly described as shared, the matched-mode, teacher-context, and
entropy analyses use independent rollout draws under this common
protocol. The model-type summary in
[Table 8](#S6.T8 "Table 8 ‣ 6.2 Privileged information Effect ‣ 6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
computes all of its metrics within the entropy-analysis draw for each
checkpoint.

The $`D_{i}^{\mathrm{fwd}}>0.05`$ statistic thresholds this summed
diagnostic and is distinct from the training-time pointwise contribution
cap. Encourage and Discourage are the fractions of positions with
sampled-token log advantage $`a_{i}>0`$ and $`a_{i}<0`$. The remainder
are ties at stored precision. Because $`y_{i}^{S}\sim\pi_{i}^{S}`$,
$`\mathbb{E}_{y_{i}^{S}\sim\pi_{i}^{S}}[a_{i}]=-D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\pi_{i}^{S}\|\pi_{i}^{T})\leq 0`$,
so student-policy sampling yields a non-positive expectation.
Measurements at initialization exclude student drift and optimizer-state
effects.

For the teacher-side context conditions of
[Table 9](#S6.T9 "Table 9 ‣ 6.2 Privileged information Effect ‣ 6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
Answer only, Unrelated solution, short and long Full solution, and
CoT+Solution share the same student rollouts (sampled under the
long-solution pool. See
[subsubsection D.1.5](#A4.SS1.SSS5 "D.1.5 Long Full-Solution Teacher Prefixes ‣ D.1 Token-Level Analysis Details ‣ Appendix D Supplementary Analysis Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).
For the position-window analysis of
[subsubsection D.1.6](#A4.SS1.SSS6 "D.1.6 Position-Window Signal by Checkpoint ‣ D.1 Token-Level Analysis Details ‣ Appendix D Supplementary Analysis Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
the protocol is identical except that completions are capped at 6,144
tokens and metrics are aggregated within fixed response-position
intervals.

#### D.1.2 Additional Matched-Mode Token-Level Signals

[Table 25](#A4.T25 "Table 25 ‣ D.1.2 Additional Matched-Mode Token-Level Signals ‣ D.1 Token-Level Analysis Details ‣ Appendix D Supplementary Analysis Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")complements

downstream OPSD outcomes.

#### D.1.3 Where the Divergence Mass Falls in the Vocabulary

The vocabulary analysis supports the mode-transfer conclusion in
[subsection 5.1](#S5.SS1 "5.1 Valid Comparison Setting ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
large cumulative divergence contributions often come from punctuation,
delimiters, the frequent word the, LaTeX markers, and
reasoning-transition tokens such as “Let,” “So,” “We,” and “I.”

Tables [26](#A4.T26 "Table 26 ‣ D.1.4 Teacher-Side Context Signal by Checkpoint ‣ D.1 Token-Level Analysis Details ‣ Appendix D Supplementary Analysis Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
and [27](#A4.T27 "Table 27 ‣ D.1.4 Teacher-Side Context Signal by Checkpoint ‣ D.1 Token-Level Analysis Details ‣ Appendix D Supplementary Analysis Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
report seven context conditions for ten checkpoints;

results support the context finding in
[subsection 5.3](#S5.SS3 "5.3 Privileged Information ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
context type shapes the signal. Within OpenThoughts, CoT+Solution

#### D.1.5 Long Full-Solution Teacher Prefixes

The main teacher-context analysis uses the 1,024-token prompt cap from
training. The length control rescores the same student rollouts with a
12,288-token full-solution teacher prefix. Answer only and Unrelated
solution retain the short budget, and token-level CoT+Solution uses a
10,240-token cap. All five prefixes share identical student completions
sampled from the long-solution pool, with the short condition truncating
$`C`$ in $`Q\oplus C`$. This offline control differs from the training
intervention in
[subsection 5.3](#S5.SS3 "5.3 Privileged Information ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
which adds a separate CoT field to matched OpenThoughts rows and uses an
8,192-token teacher cap.

Consistent with
[subsection 5.3](#S5.SS3 "5.3 Privileged Information ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
prefix length alone changes the initial signal only slightly; the Answer
only $`<`$ Unrelated solution $`<`$ Full solution ordering remains
unchanged
([Table 28](#A4.T28 "Table 28 ‣ D.1.5 Long Full-Solution Teacher Prefixes ‣ D.1 Token-Level Analysis Details ‣ Appendix D Supplementary Analysis Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

## Appendix E Additional Experiments and Analyses

### E.1 Cross-Dataset Teacher-Context Replication

We repeat the solution-only versus CoT-plus-solution comparison on two
disjoint OpenMathReasoning subsets using Qwen3-1.7B, with identical
examples and row order within each pair. More detailed teacher context
does not necessarily improve training, as in
[subsection 5.3](#S5.SS3 "5.3 Privileged Information ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
adding CoT lowers Avg@8 from 34.4 to 27.8 on the at-most-1k subset and
from 34.7 to 29.0 on the 2–4k subset despite the larger teacher prompt
budget
([Table 54](#A7.T54 "Table 54 ‣ G.1.4.1 OpenMathReasoning Replication. ‣ G.1.4 Teacher-Side Context ‣ G.1 Main-Text Experiment Breakdowns ‣ Appendix G Per-Dataset Evaluation Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

### E.2 Instruction-Only Teacher Prefixes

We remove privileged teacher content and vary only the English student
and teacher instructions. Concise-to-detailed (C$`\to`$D) asks the
student for a concise solution and the teacher for a detailed one.
D$`\to`$C reverses the instructions.

| Model | Base | Full solution | No priv.: C$`\to`$D | No priv.: D$`\to`$C |
|----|----|----|----|----|
| Qwen3-1.7B | 34.0 / 59.2 / 18.6k | 35.3 / 60.8 / 22.4k | 23.1 / 46.7 / 12.6k | 36.3 / 62.5 / 25.0k |
| OLMo-3-7B-Think | 63.5 / 81.7 / 18.7k | 58.8 / 77.5 / 21.7k | 60.7 / 80.8 / 16.5k | 56.4 / 75.0 / 24.1k |

Table 32: Instruction-only prefix controls after 100 OPSD steps. Each
cell reports Avg@8 / Pass@8 / mean output length. Per-dataset results
are in
[Table 77](#A7.T77 "Table 77 ‣ G.3.4 Additional Training Controls ‣ G.3 Additional-Experiment Breakdowns ‣ Appendix G Per-Dataset Evaluation Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models").

Teacher context shapes OPSD beyond privileged semantics, extending
[subsection 5.3](#S5.SS3 "5.3 Privileged Information ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
instruction-only prefixes produce direction-dependent changes without
privileged content.

### E.3 Additional Training Controls

#### E.3.1 Pointwise Contribution-Clip Threshold Sweep

We vary only the pointwise vocabulary-contribution cap, retraining
Qwen3-1.7B and OLMo-3-7B-Think with
$`\tau_{\mathrm{clip}}\in\{0.01,0.1,0.2\}`$ while keeping all other
settings fixed, and compare against Base and the default
$`\tau_{\mathrm{clip}}=0.05`$ run

#### E.4.1 The NTh Mode Is Not Strictly Isolated

Reasoning mode remains a confound rather than a clean semantic control,
as in
[subsection 5.1](#S5.SS1 "5.1 Valid Comparison Setting ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models").
Specifically, Qwen3.5-4B sometimes emits an additional `</think>`
delimiter after the chat template closes the empty thinking block and
produces longer outputs than Qwen3-4B. The delimiter persists after
full-parameter NTh/NTh training, so the two modes are not operationally
isolated.

#### E.4.2 The Distinguishing Signal Is Dynamic, Not Initial

Consistent with
[subsection 6.2](#S6.SS2 "6.2 Privileged information Effect ‣ 6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
initial KL does not predict training stability. Matched modes start with
lower forward KL than their corresponding mismatches, yet both later
collapse; the subsequent entropy and forward-KL rise separates
Qwen3.5-4B from the bounded Qwen3 controls
([Table 37](#A5.T37 "Table 37 ‣ E.4.3 LoRA Attenuates the Drift ‣ E.4 LoRA Diagnostics and Qwen3.5 Collapse ‣ Appendix E Additional Experiments and Analyses ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
[Figure 9](#A5.F9 "Figure 9 ‣ E.4.3 LoRA Attenuates the Drift ‣ E.4 LoRA Diagnostics and Qwen3.5 Collapse ‣ Appendix E Additional Experiments and Analyses ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

#### E.4.3 LoRA Attenuates the Drift

Parameter restriction attenuates but does not remove the collapse
behavior. The LoRA controls use rank 64 with scale 128 on q_proj,
k_proj, v_proj, o_proj, gate_proj, up_proj, and down_proj; all other
parameters are frozen. LoRA removes length growth but leaves an accuracy
drop for NTh/NTh, while Th/Th still collapses
([Figure 9](#A5.F9 "Figure 9 ‣ E.4.3 LoRA Attenuates the Drift ‣ E.4 LoRA Diagnostics and Qwen3.5 Collapse ‣ Appendix E Additional Experiments and Analyses ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

Figure 9: Matched-mode training dynamics for full-parameter and LoRA
Qwen3.5-4B versus three Qwen3 full-parameter controls. Curves are
five-step moving averages from step 2 onward. Step 1 contains a one-off
startup batch and is omitted.

| S   | T   | Fwd. KL | $`D>.05`$ | Agree. | Enc. | Disc. |
|-----|-----|---------|-----------|--------|------|-------|
| NTh | NTh | 0.087   | 17.7      | 94.7   | 34.1 | 63.4  |
| NTh | Th  | 0.208   | 31.2      | 92.7   | 14.9 | 84.6  |
| Th  | NTh | 0.140   | 27.2      | 92.0   | 56.0 | 43.4  |
| Th  | Th  | 0.066   | 16.5      | 95.0   | 40.2 | 58.7  |

[Table 7](#S6.T7 "Table 7 ‣ 6.1 Mismatched Mode Effect ‣ 6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models").

[Table 49](#A7.T49 "Table 49 ‣ G.1.1 Student–Teacher Reasoning-Mode Alignment ‣ G.1 Main-Text Experiment Breakdowns ‣ Appendix G Per-Dataset Evaluation Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models").

### E.5 Replications of Recent OPSD Variants

We reproduce two recent OPSD variants: Purified OPSD replaces the raw
privileged-teacher target with a PMI-corrected target, while
$`\beta`$-OPSD uses an interpolated teacher–reference target with
return-to-go credit assignment. We test whether vanilla OPSD’s model and
configuration sensitivity persists under these targets.

Purified OPSD uses the authors’ main PMI setting: LoRA rank 64 (scaling
128), learning rate $`5\times 10^{-6}`$, 200 updates, global batch 32,
1,024-token prompt and rollout caps, symmetric JSD, PMI strength 1, and
PMI soft-clip threshold 10. We evaluate answer-only and full-solution
fields. $`\beta`$-OPSD uses the same rank, learning rate, step count,
batch size, and caps; its teacher weight increases from 0.5 to 0.8, its
reference is the stop-gradient current student, and its return-to-go
discount is 0.99. All rows use the four-benchmark, eight-sample
protocol.

|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Variant | Model | Privileged field | Avg@8 | $`\Delta_{\mathrm{B/O}}`$ | Pass@8 | $`\Delta_{\mathrm{B/O}}`$ | Length | $`\Delta_{\mathrm{B/O}}`$ |
| Purified OPSD | Qwen3-1.7B | Answer | 39.9 | $`+5.9/+4.6`$ | 63.3 | $`+4.1/+2.5`$ | 24.2k | $`+29.7/+7.9`$ |
|  | Qwen3-1.7B | Solution | 37.5 | $`+3.5/+2.2`$ | 65.0 | $`+5.8/+4.2`$ | 20.8k | $`+11.5/-7.2`$ |
|  | Qwen3-4B | Answer | 63.3 | $`+1.0/+5.4`$ | 79.2 | $`+0.0/+3.3`$ | 19.6k | $`+10.1/-2.0`$ |
|  | Qwen3-4B | Solution | 61.3 | $`-1.0/+3.4`$ | 77.5 | $`-1.7/+1.7`$ | 18.5k | $`+4.3/-7.1`$ |
|  | Qwen3-4B-Thinking | Answer | 75.1 | $`-0.3/+9.3`$ | 84.2 | $`-2.5/+0.8`$ | 24.8k | $`+8.8/-42.9`$ |
|  | Qwen3-4B-Thinking | Solution | 75.3 | $`-0.1/+9.5`$ | 86.7 | $`+0.0/+3.3`$ | 24.2k | $`+6.2/-44.2`$ |
|  | OLMo-3-7B-Think | Answer | 64.5 | $`+1.0/+5.7`$ | 80.8 | $`-0.9/+3.3`$ | 19.2k | $`+2.4/-11.7`$ |

| $`\beta`$-OPSD | Qwen3-1.7B | Solution | 25.1 | $`-8.9/-10.2`$ | 49.2 | $`-10.0/-11.7`$ | 13.4k | $`-28.0/-40.0`$ |

#### F.1.1 Maximum Student Rollout Length

The seed repetitions support the rollout-length finding in
[subsection 5.2](#S5.SS2 "5.2 Training Steps and Maximum Student Rollout Length ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
OPSD-256 has higher Avg@8 than OPSD-1024 across all training and
evaluation seeds
([Table 41](#A6.T41 "Table 41 ‣ F.1.3 Teacher-Side Context (Prefix) Interventions ‣ F.1 Seed Sensitivity ‣ Appendix F Robustness Checks ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
[Table 42](#A6.T42 "Table 42 ‣ F.1.3 Teacher-Side Context (Prefix) Interventions ‣ F.1 Seed Sensitivity ‣ Appendix F Robustness Checks ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

#### F.1.2 Loss-Token Position Selection

The seed repetitions also support the position finding in
[subsection 6.3](#S6.SS3 "6.3 Forward KL at Different Positions ‣ 6 Analysis: From Token-Level Signals to Optimization Dynamics ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
with a fixed 256-position budget, First-256 has the highest mean Avg@8
on Qwen3-1.7B, while later-position variants remain below Base on
OLMo-3-7B-Think
([Table 43](#A6.T43 "Table 43 ‣ F.1.3 Teacher-Side Context (Prefix) Interventions ‣ F.1 Seed Sensitivity ‣ Appendix F Robustness Checks ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),
[Table 44](#A6.T44 "Table 44 ‣ F.1.3 Teacher-Side Context (Prefix) Interventions ‣ F.1 Seed Sensitivity ‣ Appendix F Robustness Checks ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

The seed repetitions support the context finding in
[subsection 5.3](#S5.SS3 "5.3 Privileged Information ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"):
Answer only does not consistently lead, and Unrelated solution remains
comparable to Full solution
([Table 45](#A6.T45 "Table 45 ‣ F.1.3 Teacher-Side Context (Prefix) Interventions ‣ F.1 Seed Sensitivity ‣ Appendix F Robustness Checks ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models"),

### F.2 Temperature Robustness Checks

We vary the evaluation decoding temperature, student rollout-sampling
temperature, and teacher scoring temperature while holding all other
settings fixed.

#### F.2.1 Decoding Temperature

The decoding-temperature check leaves the model-dependent conclusion in
[subsection 5.5](#S5.SS5 "5.5 Initial Model Ability ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
unchanged: small Instruct-model gains can reverse, while
Qwen3-4B-Thinking degrades at both temperatures with substantial length
growth
([Table 47](#A6.T47 "Table 47 ‣ F.2.1 Decoding Temperature ‣ F.2 Temperature Robustness Checks ‣ Appendix F Robustness Checks ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

Training-temperature changes likewise provide no consistent remedy:
lowering both temperatures preserves accuracy while shortening outputs,
whereas asymmetric settings do not improve accuracy consistently
([Table 48](#A6.T48 "Table 48 ‣ F.2.2 Training Sampling and Scoring Temperatures ‣ F.2 Temperature Robustness Checks ‣ Appendix F Robustness Checks ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")).

#### G.1.2 Maximum Student Rollout Length

Dataset Avg. gives the solid-curve points and dashed Base levels in
[Figure 3](#S5.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ 5.2 Training Steps and Maximum Student Rollout Length ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models").
Th/Th and NTh/NTh denote matched student–teacher modes. The NTh/NTh
Qwen3 length sweeps are a diagnostic exception to the native Th/Th
setting used for subsequent hybrid-model conclusions: they test whether
the length trend also appears when the same hybrid checkpoints are
operated in NTh mode. The 1024 / Uni-256 and 1024 / Final-256 rows keep
the rollout cap at 1,024 tokens and apply the loss to at most 256
positions. Uniform samples without replacement, whereas Final retains
the last positions. Both retain every valid position in shorter
completions. The sweep curves exclude these position controls.
[Table 2](#S5.T2 "Table 2 ‣ 5.2 Training Steps and Maximum Student Rollout Length ‣ 5 Experimental Results ‣ Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models")
averages the Qwen3-1.7B and OLMo-3-7B-Think results.

from 34.7 to 29.0 on the 2–4k subset
